Document 346: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

### Page 14

14
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
From Follow the Money and Greenberg Center for Geoeconomics
export. The world has never encountered such elastic
supply.
**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy
differences (TRQs vs. a flat tariff, for example) the

harder it would be to ever develop a harmonized
policy approach. But with President Trump, a
harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

Document 347: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integratingEurope’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
Eurogroup and Economic and Financial Affairs Council (ECOFIN) meetings (9 November)
Source: Jill Beckmann on Unsplash
New Zealand general election (7 November)
COP 31 in Türkiye (9-20 November)
3

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after
strong recent state-level performances by Barisan Nasional (BN). The contest will also be watched for
its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as “not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
49th ASEAN Summit (16-18 November)
Source: istockphoto
EU-UK Summit (20 November)
Melaka state election (14 November)

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
The upcoming local elections could serve as a political barometer ahead of the 2028 general election–
but only up to a point. Local elections in Taiwan are primarily driven by municipal ‘bread and butter’
economic issues; geopolitics and cross-Strait relations have historically not been at the forefront of

voters’ concerns. While this divergence limits theelections’ predictive value for the January 2028 general
vote, the results can provide a sense of the domestic balance of power.
Elections in Taiwan’s six special municipalities – Taipei, New Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, Taoyuan, and
Kaohsiung – will serve as particularly important watchpoints. Some recent polling suggests a potentially
close contest in Taipei between incumbent mayor Chiang Wan-an (Kuomintang) and legislator Shen Po-

yang (Democratic Progressive Party).
Spain’s snap general election could mark a major turning point for Spanish politics and the EU,
potentially ending eight years of Socialist-led government under Pedro Sánchez and bringing the
conservative People’s Party (PP) to power with support from the far-right Vox. Polling suggests PP is
ahead and a PP-Vox alignment is the only one projected to reach a majority. A rightward shift in one of
the EU’s largest economies could occur just as Europe enters a wider electoral cycle, potentially

complicating consensus on migration, climate policy, Ukraine and European integration.
The Forum comes at a pivotal moment in EU-Latin American relations. It is the EU’s first big commercial
push into Argentina since the EU-Mercosur trade deal began to be provisionally applied on 1 May. The
agreement opens new opportunities for trade, investment and market access. The EU is Argentina’s
largest source of foreign direct investment. Around 800 government, business and financial leaders will

focus on energy, critical minerals, agriculture and infrastructure. The EU’s Global Gateway investment
agenda provides a framework for mobilizing public and private capital. While a key outcome will be the
launch of a structured EU-Argentina investment dialogue, to watch will be the ability of Argentine
officials to allay concerns around policy stability and regulatory predictability.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order

Source: Nestor Barbitta on Unsplash

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
A potential Trump-Kim meeting in November could restart official bilateral dialogue, though North
Korea remains highly unlikely to abandon its nuclear program. South Korea’s National Intelligence
Service said in late September that it saw a roughly 50% chance of US-North Korea talks around the
APEC Summit in Shenzhen, although more recent reporting suggests the prospects may have

diminished. If talks do resume, they would only mark the first step in a long and highly uncertain
diplomatic process.
As we noted earlier this year, North Korea is highly unlikely to agree to denuclearization. Unless renewed
negotiations sidestep denuclearization in favor of a risk-reduction or coexistence framework, dialogue is
likely to stall, not least given Pyongyang’s constitutional pledge to continue expanding its nuclear
arsenal. This would make even a negotiated freeze on further expansion difficult to achieve.

Document 468: What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

### Page 15

Document 540: ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～.pdf

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 27 日

1/5 
ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか 
～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日の大統領選挙の決選投票において現職のルラ大統領が逆転勝利を
収めたことを受けて、 翌26 日の金融市場では株式、 通貨レアル、 国債が同時に売られる 「ト
リプル安」に直面した。背景には、右派フラビオ氏の勝利による財政健全化や金利低下への
期待を過度に織り込んだ取引が巻き戻されたことに加え、ルラ政権下で財政健全化の取り組
みが遅れるとの懸念がある。 
○ 同国では、政府債務の大きさや義務的経費の割合の高さが財政運営の制約となるなか、レア
ル安によるインフレ圧力の高まりが中銀の追加利下げ余地を狭めるとともに、金利上昇が景
気や財政を一段と圧迫するリスクが懸念される。一方、十分な外貨準備など対外ショックに
対する一定の耐性を有するほか、当局も市場安定化策を講じており、過度な相場変動が落ち
着く余地はある。 
○ ただし、市場の信認回復には具体的な財政健全化への取り組みが不可欠である。今後は、次
期政権の経済チームの人事や2027 年度予算の内容、 議会との合意形成を通じて、 財政健全化
に向けた道筋を示せるかが重要となる。

### Page 2

2/5 
【ルラ氏の逆転勝利を受けて、金融市場においてブラジル資産を売却する動きが広がる】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）
から出馬した現職のルラ大統領が、 右派
ＰＬ（自由党）のフラビオ・ボルソナロ
上院議員を破り、再選を決めた（注１）。
ブラジル高等選挙裁判所 （ＴＳＥ） が公
表した最終結果によれば、 ルラ氏の得票
率は 51.79％、フラビオ氏は 48.21％と
なった。これを受けて、翌 26 日の金融
市場では、株式、通貨レアル、国債のす
べてに売り圧力が強まった（図１）。 株式
市場では、 主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）
が前営業日比で一時▲17％以上下落し、
取引を一時停止するサーキットブレーカーが発動された。 為替市場においてもレアルが大きく下落
するとともに、 債券市場では国債利回りや金利先物市場での金利上昇が進むなど、 「トリプル安」 に
直面した。

【右派政権への交代を過度に織り込んだ取引の巻き戻しが、相場変動を増幅させた】 
市場が大幅に変動した背景には、決選投票の前に積み上がった政権交代への期待が剥落したこと
がある。10 月４日の第１回投票では、事前予想に反してフラビオ氏が首位となった（注２） 。これ
を受けて、その後の市場では、同氏の勝利によって財政健全化が進み、将来的な金利低下につなが
るとの期待が強まる動きがみられた （注３）。 決選投票直前には、 ボベスパ指数は第１回投票前に比
べて約７％、レアルも対ドルで約５％上昇していた。 
投資家の間では、フラビオ氏の勝利を受けた財政規律の強化や経済改革が進む可能性を織り込み、
株式やレアルを買うとともに、金利低下を見込む取引を進めていた。しかし、ルラ氏の逆転勝利に
よってこうした前提が崩れ、複数の市場で同時に持ち高を調整する動きが強まったと考えられる。
よって、現職の続投という結果にもかかわらず、市場が政権交代を強く見込んでいたことで、大幅
な価格調整に繋がった。今回の下落幅を考える際には、ルラ政権の政策に対する評価に加え、選挙
前の取引が特定の結果に偏っていた可能性を考慮する必要がある。このため、市場では右派政権に
よる政策転換への期待が過度に先行していたと考えられる。

注１ 10 月26 日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666866.html） 
注２ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html） 
注３ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html）

### Page 3

3/5

【市場が抱く懸念には、公的財務の大きさに加え、 硬直的な歳出増による財政運営の難しさがある】 
市場がルラ氏の再選に厳しい反応を示した背景には、社会給付などの拡充が続くことにより、財
政健全化の取り組みが 遅れるとの懸念
がある。2026 年８月末時点における一般
政府の債務残高 （グロス値）はＧＤＰ比
82.9％に達している。さらに、ここ数年
の同国では、 インフレによる高金利が常
態化するなか、利払い負担の大きさが財
政運営を難しくしている。さらに、 近年
は司法判断に基づく支払債務 （プレカト
リオス）の増大も歳出を圧迫しており、
財政運営の硬直性を一段と強める要因
となっている。 
こうした状況では、 新たな恒久歳出に見合う財源を確保できるかが重要になる。 財政の持続可能
性に対する懸念が強まれば、投資家は国債の保有により高い利回りを求める。この結果、政府の新
規調達や借換えに伴う負担が増え、財政健全化を一段と困難にする可能性がある。株式、通貨、債
券が同時に売られたことは、 将来の政策運営に対する不確実性が、 ブラジル資産全体に求められる
リスクの上乗せ幅を拡大させたと解釈できる。 
財政運営の硬直性は、2027 年度予算案において、 基礎的歳出に占める義務的経費の割合が91.7％
に上ることにも現れている。このため、 裁量的な歳出削減による財政健全化の実現のハードルは極
めて高いと考えられる。今後は、政府が掲げる財政目標に加え、前述したプレカトリオスなど例外
措置を含めた実際の歳出、恒久財源の確保、債務残高の見通しを確認することが重要になる。ルラ
政権が主導する社会政策の効果を見極めつつ、給付の対象や制度設計、税制優遇、支出の優先順位
を見直すことができるかが、財政運営への信認を左右することが予想される。

【レアル安が利下げ余地を狭める一方、金利上昇が景気と財政を圧迫する懸念も残る】 
今回の混乱が、 金融政策の先行きに影響を与える可能性にも注意が必要である。 中銀は９月の定
例会合（COPOM）において、政策金利である Selic を 13.75％に引き下げた（注４）。一方で、会合
後に公表された声明においては、 持続的な通貨下落やインフレ期待の不安定化、 消費刺激策などを
物価の上振れリスクに挙げるとともに、 財政政策が金融政策や資産価格に及ぼす影響を注視する姿
勢を示した。

注４ ９月17 日付レポート「ブラジル中銀は５会合連続の利下げも、緩和継続には慎重姿勢」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/665843.html）

### Page 4

4/5 
足元では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高などを背景に、鈍化基調が続いてきたインフレ率が
再び加速に転じる兆しがみられる（図
３） 。 こうしたなか、レアル安の動きが長
期化すれば、 輸入品や輸入原材料の価格
上昇を通じて、 物価への押し上げ圧力が
強まることが予想される。 さらに、 企業
や家計 部門における インフレ予想が上
方修正されれば、 中銀が追加利下げを進
める余地は狭まる。次回の COPOM は 11
月３～４日に予定されており、 選挙後の
為替変動や期待インフレの動きをどう
評価するかが注目される。 中銀として
は、レアル安の継続性のほか、 物価への波及、 国内需要の減速などを総合的に判断する必要がある。 
実体経済への影響としては、 企業と家計の資金調達環境が厳しくなることが懸念される。 企業の
借入費用や債務の借換え負担が増えれば、 利益や設備投資に下押し圧力がかかる。家計向け融資の
金利上昇は、 住宅や耐久消費財の需要を抑える要因となる。こうした金融環境の悪化が長期化すれ
ば、 景気減速による税収の伸び悩みと利払い負担の増加が重なり、 財政運営をさらに難しくするこ
とも考えられる。 財政運営に対する懸念が金利を押し上げるとともに、 その金利上昇が景気と財政
を圧迫するという循環を抑えられるかが、今後の重要な課題となる。

【当局の市場安定化策と対外面での「緩衝材」はあるが、財政運営への信認回復が不可欠】 
市場の混乱を受けて、 当局は国債市場の安定化に向けた対応を進めている。26 日には固定利付国
債や物価連動国債の買い戻しを実施するとともに、27 日に予定していた物価連動国債と29 日の固
定利付国債の入札を中止する一方、27 日の政策金利連動国債の入札は維持した。これらの措置は、
売り注文が集中した市場において買い手を確保する一方、 急激な価格変動を抑えることに役立つと
考えられる。ただし、その効果を持続させるには、中期的な財政改善策を示すとともに、投資家が
国債を保有しやすい環境を整えることが必要になる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
一方で、対外的な観点でみればブラジルには「緩衝材」が存在している。９月末時点における外
貨準備高は3,054 億ドルに上る。 この水
準は、ＩＭＦ（国際通貨基金）が国際金
融市場の動揺への耐性の有無の基準と
して示すＡＲＡ（適正水準評価）に照ら
して 「適正 （100～150％） 」 の域内にとど
まる（図４） 。そのうえ、９月までの 12
ヵ月累計の経常赤字は600 億ドルである
のに対し、対内直接投資の純流入は 883
億ドルとなっている。 これらの数字は選
挙前までの実績である ことに注意が必
要であるものの、 ブラジルが市場の急変
に対応するうえで一定の基盤を持つことを示唆している。ＩＭＦが７月に公表したⅣ条協議 （対ブ
ラジル審査） レポートにおいても、 変動相場制と十分な外貨準備が外部ショックを吸収する手段に
なると評価している。先行きについては、こうした基盤を踏まえつつ、財政不安と高金利が長期化
するリスクを見極める必要がある。

【先行きは次期政権における経済チームの人事、2027 年予算、議会との合意形成に要注意】 
今後注目すべき点は、 次期政権の経済チームの人選と、 大統領が財政改善策をどの程度政治的に
支えるかである。財務相などの人事は、政策運営の方向を示す材料となるとともに、その後に示さ
れる具体的な歳出・歳入措置も重要になる。そのうえで、2027 年予算案の修正、 新たな給付や支援
策の財源、義務的経費や税制優遇の見直しなどが、信認回復の判断材料となり得る。 
議会との関係も政策の実行可能性を左右することは避けられない。 次期下院においては、フラビ
オ氏が所属するＰＬが 121 議席と最大勢力となり、ＰＴを中心とする政党連合が 88 議席を獲得す
る見通しである。 ルラ政権としては、 予算や制度改正を進めるために他党との合意形成を図る必要
がある。右派勢力の影響力は、急激な歳出拡大を抑える要因となり得る。一方で、予算配分や税制
を巡る交渉が難航すれば、 財政健全化策の成立も遅れる可能性がある。 議会構成だけで財政規律の
改善を判断せず、 具体的な政策についてどのような合意が成立するかを確認することが重要である。 
現時点においては、 選挙前に積み上がった取引の巻き戻しが一巡すれば、 急激な値動きが落ち着
く余地はあると見込まれる。 しかし、 選挙前の相場を支えていた財政改善や金利低下への期待を取
り戻すには、政策面での裏付けが不可欠である。ルラ政権にとっては、有権者が期待する生活改善
の要求に応える一方、歳出の優先順位と財源を明確にしつつ、 政府債務の拡大を抑える道筋を示す
ことが求められる。

### Page 1

中⾦公司于 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇在第三批 “ 证券、基⾦、保险公司互换便利 ” 项下完成股票买⼊。同⽇，
国泰海通表⽰，将继续利⽤互换便利⼯具，增持 A 股股票及股票 ETF 。银河证券亦完成第三
批互换便利操作下的第⼀笔回购融资及⾸批投资买⼊。
摘要：
中国央⾏互换便利⼯具（ SFISF ）第三批操作加速落地，中⾦、中国银河证券、国泰海通等多家
券商已率先完成⼊市买⼊动作。
据证券时报最新报道，中⾦公司于 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇在第三批 “ 证券、基⾦、保险公司互换便利 ” 项下完成
股票买⼊。中⾦公司相关负责⼈表⽰，下⼀步，中⾦公司将继续使⽤互换便利业务获得的资⾦
进⼀步增持股票，助⼒资本市场健康稳定发展。
与此同时，国泰海通第三批 “ 证券、基⾦、保险公司互换便利（ SFISF ） ” 业务完成前期准备⼯
作，已开展回购融资交易，并完成⾸批股票买⼊操作。国泰海通表⽰，将继续利⽤互换便利⼯
具，增持 A 股股票及股票 ETF 。
银河证券亦完成第三批互换便利操作下的第⼀笔回购融资及⾸批投资买⼊。两家机构均表⽰，

后续将持续运⽤融⼊资⾦进⾏权益市场配置。
此次第三批操作于 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇启动，共有 31 家券商和基⾦公司参与并中标，整体意向申报额度超过
1000 亿元，⽆论参与机构数量还是申报规模均明显超过前两次。市场认为，在外部环境复杂多
变、市场波动加剧的背景下，机构踊跃参与有助于推动增量资⾦⼊市、修复市场⻛险偏好。
多家机构确认中标，部分规模已披露
据澎湃新闻不完全统计，包括中⾦公司、国泰海通、中信证券、中信建投、招商证券、⼴发证
券、东⽅财富证券、东⽅证券、国联⺠⽣证券、东吴证券、平安证券、⻓城证券在内的 12 家券
商确认参与申报了本次操作。
其中，国泰海通、中信证券、招商证券、⼴发证券、东⽅证券、国联⺠⽣证券、东吴证券、⻓
城证券等 8 家机构已向记者确认中标。
规模⽅⾯，国泰海通、中信证券均中标 100 亿元，东吴证券中标 50 亿元，⻓城证券中标 20 亿元。
多家机构同时向记者表达了对中国资本市场中⻓期前景的看好态度。
规模与参与度均超前两批
华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道， SFISF 由中国⼈⺠银⾏于 2024 年 10 ⽉创设，核⼼机制是⽀持符合条件的

证券、基⾦、保险公司以债券、股票 ETF 、沪深 300 成分股等资产为抵押，换⼊国债、央⾏票据
等⾼等级流动性资产，所获资⾦须专项⽤于股票及股票 ETF 的投资和做市，不得挪作他⽤。
⾃创设以来， SFISF 已先后于 2024 年 10 ⽉及 2025 年 1 ⽉完成两次操作，规模分别为 500 亿元和
550 亿元，两次均有 20 家机构参与投标。
此次第三批操作意向申报额度超过 1000 亿元，参与机构达 31 家，参与家数和申报规模均创新
⾼。据证券时报援引参会⼈⼠报道，这表明该⼯具在稳定资本市场⽅⾯已形成⼀定托底效果，
获得机构的⼴泛认可。

面板及零組件 
友達 (2409 TT)

維持評等  
中立(Neutral) 
面板需求持續下滑，本業虧損擴大  
⚫ 三大業務獲利顯著衰退  
⚫ 營運展望承壓，面板需求持續 下滑  
⚫ 公司持續推動轉型，押注非面板前瞻技術  
3Q26毛利率下滑，受產品組合及消費性需求疲弱 ：公司於 2026年 10
月 30日舉行法說會，公告 3Q26財務數據，營收 669億元 (QoQ -5.7%，
YoY-4.3%)，毛利率 10.1%(QoQ -2.9ppts，YoY+0.5ppts)，營業淨利率
-3.5%(QoQ -3.8ppts，YoY-0.9ppts)，營業外利益 3.65 億元，單季 EPS 
-0.33元。3Q26  Display、Mobility 及 Vertical 業務營收占比分別為 46%、
30%、20%，主要衰退來自 Display 業務，因品牌廠上半年 提前拉貨結
束，且 Monitor/NB 持續受記憶體與 IC缺貨影響，需求持續放緩，客
戶拉貨持續持保守態度，Mobility Solutions 及 Vertical Solutions 業務
表現相對持穩。整體 營收規模下滑， 三大業務獲利表現均較前季顯著
下滑，且公司持續在車用及新技術開發投入研究，使營業費用高於預
期，本業虧損擴大。  
4Q26 面板需求持續下滑 ，預期 Display 業務仍衰退：公司指引 4Q26
三大業務， Mobility Solutions 呈季持平，雖車市受零組件短缺及中國
內需市場保守並轉趨出口導向影響歐洲車廠，但公司車用業務仍相對
穩健； Vertical Solutions 若未考慮能源事業 併表影響下，季減低個位
數百分比；Display 業務延續 IT產品需求疲弱，客戶採購保守，短期
內記憶體及零組件缺貨影響無法緩解，預期仍呈現營收下滑 。受面板
業務持續下滑影響，我們預期 4Q26營收 638億元 (QoQ -4.59%)，毛利
率 10.46%(QoQ+0.06ppts) 。 
推動轉型，友達成立創新研究院，聚焦 前瞻技術：友達組織結構調整，
新設創新研究院，將著重在 MicroLED、光通訊、玻璃基板、 AR光波
導及低軌衛星通訊天線 。在短中長距離傳輸應用上，友達開發的光引
擎可依客戶需求提供 MicroLED 或 Micro VCSEL 技術方案，目前已與
客戶進行規格討論，並建置小量研發線，預計量產投資在一到兩年後
技術成熟時大規模展開。 而在玻璃基板方面，公司在鍍銅、 RDL佈線
及 TGV皆有技術佈局， 玻璃尺寸包含 510x515mm、310x310mm 等規
格，並持續開發相關技術。  
公司在 Micro LED 光通訊應用及玻璃基板相關技術開發仍屬初期階
段，短期尚無實質營收貢獻， 我們預估 202 6-27年 EPS為-0.47、-0.34
元，公司過往 PBR區間約為 0.6x~1 .5x，我們基於 2027年預估之每股
淨值 x1.2倍 PBR，目標價為 22.4 元，維持中立之投資評等建議。 風
險因子：面板報價出貨不如預期 、消費性電子 需求不如預期 。

投資評等說明  個股評等 Rating 定義 
買進 Buy (B) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力極具吸引力 
增加持股 Overweight (OW) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力略有吸引力 
中立 Neutral (N) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力相似 
降低持股 Underweight (UW) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力稍低 
賣出 Sell (S) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力較低 
未評等 Not Rated (NR)

### Page 2

1 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況① 景気は拡大している
ユーロ圏の4-6月期の実質GDP（確定値）は前期比+0.6%と、1-3月期（同横ばい）から反発した。需要項目別には、前期
にマイナス寄与だった純輸出が大幅なプラス寄与に転じて景気をけん引した。一方、最新9月の景況感指数は97.9と低下
に転じた。酷暑によるさまざまな調整圧力や政情不安（特にフランス）が景気の重荷になっているもよう。
（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット） （出所）欧州委員会ECFIN

### Page 3

2 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況② 生産は低迷している
ユーロ圏の6月の鉱工業生産は前月比▲0.1%と2カ月連続で減少し、均した動きでも下向きに。非耐久財が減産に転じ
て生産全体の重荷となった。一方、同月の名目輸出は同+1.2%と増勢が前月（同+0.7%）から加速し、均した動きでも上
向き。ゆえに生産の低迷は内需向けが主因であるもよう。
（注）灰色線は3カ月移動平均
（出所）ユーロスタット
鉱工業生産 名目輸出
（注）灰色線は3カ月移動平均
（出所）ユーロスタット

### Page 4

3 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況③ 消費は頭打ち
ユーロ圏の7月の小売数量は前月比▲0.6%と再び減少し、均した動きは頭打ち。一方、8月の新車販売台数は前月比
+1.4%の年率966万台と2カ月連続で増加したものの、均した水準は頭打ち。
小売数量 新車販売台数
（注）灰色線は3カ月移動平均
（出所）欧州中央銀行（ECB）

（出所）ユーロスタット

### Page 5

4 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況④ 雇用は緩やかに悪化
ユーロ圏の8月の雇用統計では、失業率が6.4%と7カ月連続で同じ水準となった一方、失業者数は前月差2.6万人増と3
カ月連続で増加しており、雇用情勢は緩やかに悪化している。失業率を主要国別に確認すると、ドイツやイタリアの失業
率の上昇が目立つ。
失業率と失業者数
（出所）ユーロスタット （出所）ユーロスタット
主要国別失業率

### Page 6

5 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況⑤ インフレは強含み
ユーロ圏の8月の消費者物価（総合ベース）は前年比+3.3%と、前月（同+2.9%）から上昇が加速した。また生産者物価
（建設除く総合）は7月時点で同+5.8%と、上昇が再び加速した。うちエネルギー価格も同+12.9%と上昇が再び加速し、強
含みで推移している。
消費者物価 生産者物価
（出所）ユーロスタット（出所）ユーロスタット

### Page 7

6 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況⑥ECBは9月理事会で金利を引き上げ
欧州中央銀行（ECB）は9月10日に政策理事会の結果を公表し、政策金利（預金ファシリティ金利）を現行の年2.25%か
ら年2.5%へ引き上げた。3%近いインフレが定着している一方、景気が底堅いことが背景。次々回の理事会の結果は10
月29日に公表される予定。
政策金利（預金ファシリティ金利） ユーロシステムのバランスシート
（出所）ECB（出所）ECB

### Page 8

7 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況⑦ 金利は上昇、株価は下落
ユーロ圏主要国の9月の長期金利は上昇した。インフレ加速に対する懸念に加えて、来年春のフランス大統領選に対す
る懸念などから長期金利は上昇した。一方、同月の株価は下落した。イラン情勢の緊迫化が続き、インフレ加速に対する
懸念が高まる中で、株は売りが先行した。
10年国債流通利回り 株価（EURO STOXX 50）
（出所）STOXX（出所）各国中銀

### Page 9

8 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ユーロ圏景気概況⑧ ユーロは対ドル・対円ともに下落
ユーロ相場 経常収支
（出所）ECB、ユーロスタット（出所）ECB
EUの通貨ユーロの9月の相場は対ドル・対円ともに下落した。対ドルでは、有事のドル買い圧力やフランス大統領選に対
する懸念などからユーロが売られた。対円では、日銀による利上げ加速観測からユーロが売られた。経常収支は、財収
支（貿易）黒字が高止まりしており、実需面からのユーロ買い圧力は安定している。

9 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
英国景気概況① 景気は拡大している
実質GDP 月次実質GDP
（出所）ONS（出所）英国立統計局（ONS）
英国の4-6月期の実質GDP（確定値）は前期比+0.5%と、1-3月期（同+0.6%）から増勢が鈍化した。需要項目別には、純
輸出が堅調だった一方、個人消費や総固定資本形成の増勢が鈍化した。一方、7月の月次実質GDPは前月比+0.4%と6
月（同+0.3%）から増勢が加速した。

### Page 11

10 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
英国景気概況② 生産は低迷している
英国の7月の鉱工業生産は前月比+0.2%と3カ月ぶりに増加したものの、均した動きは下向き。一方、同月の実質輸出は
同+4.4%と反発し、均した動きでも上向きに転じた。他方で、同月のサービス業生産は同+0.4%と3カ月連続で増加し、均
した動きでも上向き。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
美国密歇根⼤学 11 ⽉消费者信⼼指数初值从 10 ⽉的 48 升⾄ 51 ，超出市场预期的 48 。此番改
善主要集中在独⽴选⺠群体，该群体消费者信⼼创下⼀年来新⾼。约 57% 的受访者主动提
到⾼物价影响⽣活⽔平，这⼀⽐例创⾃ 5 ⽉以来新⾼。消费者预计未来⼀年的物价上涨
4.3% ，创三个⽉新低。
摘要：
受美国⺠众对个⼈财务及经济前景看法略有改善的提振，美国 11 ⽉消费者信⼼指数升⾄三个⽉
⾼位。
根据周五公布的调查，密歇根⼤学 11 ⽉消费者信⼼指数初值从 10 ⽉的 48 升⾄ 51 。⽽彭博针对经
济学家调查的预测中值为维持在 48 。
此番改善主要集中在独⽴选⺠群体中，他们报告的消费者信⼼创下⼀年来新⾼。相⽐之下，⺠
主党和共和党选⺠的信⼼均有所恶化。
⽣活成本担忧仍是许多美国⼈最关⼼的问题。约 57% 的受访者主动提到⾼物价侵蚀了他们的⽣

活⽔平，这⼀⽐例创下⾃ 5 ⽉以来的新⾼。
尽管裁员有限且经济增⻓强劲，但随着物价涨幅快于⼯资增⻓，家庭财务状况依然紧绷。⺠主
党在中期选举中的决定性胜利，反映出选⺠对经济现状的深刻不满，尤其是对⻝品、汽油和住
房⾼昂成本的抱怨。
通胀预期改善， AI 泡沫担忧浮现
未来⼀年的通胀预期有所改善，但仍处于⾼位。消费者预计未来⼀年的物价将上涨 4.3% ，创三
个⽉新低。他们还预计未来 5 ⾄ 10 年的物价年均涨幅为 3.4% ，较上⽉也有所回落。
调查负责⼈乔安妮 · 徐（ Joanne Hsu ）在⼀份声明中表⽰，除了对通胀的担忧外， “ 消费者的反
馈还揭⽰了对可能出现的 AI 泡沫⽇益增⻓的焦虑。 ”
她补充道： “ 由于最近⼏周股市波动，持有⼤量股票投资组合的消费者个⼈财务状况本⽉下降了
12% ，降⾄⾃ 4 ⽉以来的最低⽔平，⽽其他所有财富群体的财务状况均有温和改善。 ”
11 ⽉衡量当前个⼈财务状况的指标升⾄四个⾼位，同时美国⼈对耐⽤品购买条件的看法也从历
史低点回升。当前状况和预期状况指标均升⾄ 8 ⽉以来的最⾼⽔平。

本次调查期包含 10 ⽉ 20 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇的受访反馈。

Document 819: 英中銀、３年ぶりの利上げを決定 ～追加利上げは慎重に判断～

Source PDF: all report pdf/英中銀、３年ぶりの利上げを決定 ～追加利上げは慎重に判断～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 11 月６日

1/4 
英中銀、３年ぶりの利上げを決定 
～追加利上げは慎重に判断～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ 英イングランド銀行（ＢＯＥ）は11 月の金融政策委員会で、政策金利を3.75％から 4.00％
へ引き上げた。利上げは約３年３ヶ月ぶり。中東情勢の長期化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰
を受け、物価の上振れリスクが一段と高まったと判断した。委員９名全員が利上げを支持し
たが、今後の利上げ継続については意見が分かれる。 
○ 同時に発表された金融政策レポートでは、2027 年半ばの消費者物価上昇率が５％近くに達す
ると予想し、物価目標への復帰時期を先送りした。足元では中東情勢の緊張緩和への期待か
ら原油価格が下落しており、先行きの物価見通しが下方修正される余地もある。 
○ 来年２月の追加利上げを予想するが、その後も利上げを続ける必要があるかは、米中間選挙
後のイラン和平の行方と相俟って不透明だ。エネルギー高が賃金や他の物価に波及し、国内
の持続的なインフレに転化するかが今後の政策判断を左右しよう。

英イングランド銀行（ＢＯＥ）は11 月５日に結果を公表した金融政策委員会（ＭＰＣ）で、政
策金利を3.75％から4.00％へ 0.25％引き上げた。利上げは2023 年８月以来、約３年３ヶ月ぶり
となる。投票結果は８対１で、ベイリー総裁を含む８名が0.25％の利上げを支持し、マン委員が
0.50％の大幅利上げを主張した。前回９月会合では６名が据え置き、３名が利上げを主張してい
たが、この１ヶ月半の間で全てのメンバーが利上げが必要との判断に傾いた。 
声明文は「エネルギー価格の上昇に伴うインフレ圧力の高まりが、政策金利の引き上げを正当
化する水準に達した」と説明している。エネルギー価格の上昇が想定以上に長期化しており、他
の物価や賃金に波及するリスクを警戒している。同時に発表された11 月の金融政策レポート（旧
物価レポート）では、原油価格と天然ガス価格の想定が前回７月の見通し対比で大幅に上方修正
された。９月に前年比＋3.5％に加速した消費者物価は、来年１～３月期には同＋4.5％前後、４
～６月期には５％近くに達すると見込む。 
これまでのところ、エネルギー価格の上昇が他の物価や賃金に波及する兆候は限定的である。
一部の企業は利益率を圧縮してコスト増を吸収しているが、こうした対応には限界がある。価格
転嫁が遅れて顕在化する可能性があるほか、物価の上振れが来年春の賃金交渉に影響する可能性

### Page 2

2/4 
がある。過去数年の高インフレを受け、家計や企業が物価の動きに敏感になっていることも、二
次的効果のリスクを高めている。新たな物価見通しでは、前回と比べてより多くの二次的効果を
見込んでいる（図表１）。

経済環境はＢＯＥの想定以上に底堅い。輸出や人工知能（ＡＩ）関連の投資に支えられて成長
率が予想を上回り、個人消費も実質所得の目減りに比べて底堅い。労働市場にも安定化の兆しが
みられ、賃金やサービス物価の鈍化はこのところ停滞している。ＢＯＥは経済の余剰供給力（ス
ラック）がなお存在し、金融政策も引き締め的であると判断しているが、物価を目標に復帰させ
るのに十分かどうかの不確実性が高まっている。こうした景気や雇用の底堅さも、ＢＯＥの利上
げを後押しした。 
11 月の金融政策レポートでは、消費者物価の中心見通しを大幅に上方修正し、2026 年 10～12
月期に前年比＋3.8％（７月の前回見通しは同＋3.2％） 、2027 年10～12 月期が同＋4.0％（前回は
同＋2.1％） 、2028 年 10～12 月期が同＋2.0％（前回は同＋1.9％） 、2029 年 10～12 月期に同＋
1.8％と予想する（図表２）。２％の物価目標への復帰時期は2028 年７～９月期と、前回見通しか
ら約半年先送りされた。実質ＧＤＰ成長率の中心見通しは、2026 年 10～12 月期に同＋2.2％と成
長軌道を維持するものの、2027 年10～12 月期に同＋1.0％、2028 年 10～12 月期に同＋1.4％と、
来年はエネルギー高や金融引き締めの効果浸透により減速を予想する。

### Page 3

3/4 
こうした見通しは、金融市場が想定する金利経路を前提に作成されている。先物市場が織り込
む政策金利は2027 年末、2028 年末とも4.9％程度となり、前回を大幅に上回る。市場は25bp刻み
で約４回の追加利上げを織り込んでいる。こうした中心見通しに加え、エネルギー価格がさらに
高止まりし、二次的波及も強まる「深刻シナリオ」と、景気の減速で二次的波及が生じない「穏
健シナリオ」に基づく見通しも公表した。2027 年末の物価上昇率は前者で＋5.5％、後者で＋
3.6％と、中心見通しの＋4.0％を挟んで大きな幅がある。

ＭＰＣメンバーの個別意見でも、先行きの利上げ幅については温度差がある。マン委員はイン
フレ期待の定着を防ぐため、今回の会合で0.50％の引き上げを求めた。ピル委員やグリーン委員
も追加引き締めの必要性を強調する。また、ロンバルデリ副総裁も、物価圧力が弱まらなければ
近い将来に追加利上げが必要との立場を採る。一方、ハト派のテイラー委員とディングラ委員
は、今後の利上げ継続に慎重姿勢を崩していない。中間派のベイリー総裁は、追加利上げの合理
性を認めながらも、先行きの判断はエネルギー価格と国内インフレの動向次第とし、長期の連続
利上げを予定しているわけではないと説明した。最近の原油価格の下落を好材料とする一方、中
東情勢の解決に進展がなければ、追加利上げが必要になり得るとしている。 
金融市場では今回の0.25％の利上げはほぼ織り込み済みだったが、全メンバーが利上げを支持
した投票結果と物価見通しの上方修正は予想以上にタカ派的と受け止められた。ただ、利上げを

中心見通し 深刻シナリオ 穏健シナリオ（％）

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
決定した一方で、米国の中間選挙の結果を受け、中東情勢の緊張が緩和に向かうとの期待感か
ら、原油価格は金融政策レポートの前提を下回って下落している。市場では追加利上げが相応に
織り込まれているが、市場が織り込むほど大幅な追加利上げは必要ない可能性がある。ＢＯＥが
政策金利をどこまで引き上げるかについては不確実性が大きい。 
前回会合後の商品市況の動きからは、今回の利上げ決定には合理性がある。二次的波及が明確
になってから対応するのでは、賃金・物価の高い伸びが定着し、より大幅な利上げを迫られる恐
れがあるためだ。ただ、英国経済には実質所得の減少、住宅ローン金利の上昇、財政引き締めと
いう逆風がある。物価の高止まりで利下げが遅れたＢＯＥは、欧州中央銀行（ＥＣＢ）などと比
べて十分に政策金利を引き下げないまま利上げを再開した。インフレ圧力の高まりを受け、来年
２月の金融政策レポートの発表に合わせて、25bpの追加利上げを実施すると予想する。その後も
利上げを続ける必要があるかを巡っては、米中間選挙後のイラン情勢の変化やエネルギー価格の
動向などを巡って不透明な要素も多い。エネルギー価格の上昇が一時的な物価押し上げにとどま
るのか、国内の持続的なインフレに転化するのかが、今後の金融政策を左右しよう。

新 AI 機櫃量產，AI 投資重心擴散至整體基礎設施 
OCP（開放運算計畫）全球峰會新世代 AI 機櫃量產啟動，今年的焦點從展示
新產品轉為確認量產時程與零組件規格升級。AI 投資重心已全面由單純 GPU
晶片，快速擴散至電力、液冷散熱與資料中心營建等整體基礎設施。 
出刊日：2026/10/30 
305 期

### Page 2

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
談股論金

本期內容： 
1. 資料中心為何成為
反對目標 
2. CSP 自建發電設備
與表後供電 
3. 電網需求暴增，供
應鏈受惠

重點摘要 
■ 2026 年 OCP 大會新系統不多，市場
焦點轉向量產節奏 。輝達 VR200 機櫃
開始小量出貨，Groq 3 LPX 機櫃預計
2026 年底或 2027 年初登場，獨立的 
Vera CPU 機櫃則落在 2027 年第 1 季
末或第 2 季初。AMD Helios 機櫃設
計大致不變，預期 2027 年第 1 季開
始放量。

■ 微軟 Maia 300 代表雲端巨頭自研晶
片進入規模化階段，單一機櫃含  96 
顆加速器，功耗約167 kW，並宣稱每
MW 產出的 token 比領先的 GPU 系
統多 40%。輝達持續升級機櫃零組件，
包括 PTFE 電路板材料、超高密度連
接器 UDI 與新一代快速接頭 ZQD，帶
動電路板 、 連接器 、 散熱零件的規格與
單價提升。

■ 這波行情的受惠者從晶片廠擴大到機
櫃周邊供應鏈。投資人宜把焦點放在
已進入試產名單、良率領先的供應
商，並留意 NVIDIA*的 Kyber 架
構延遲所造成的時程變動。

圖一：OCP 展示高階 1.6T 液冷網路交換器

資料來源：OCP，MS， 2026/10/18

▋ 目標量產時程成為主角 
OCP 大會 (Open Compute Project 
Summit) 由開放運算計畫  (OCP) 基金會
主辦的年度盛會 ， 目的是推動資料中心硬體 、
軟體與 AI 基礎設施的開源與規格標準化 。
簡單說 ， 就是讓各家廠商共用同一套規格 ，
降低資料中心的建置成本。 
今年的 OCP 大會上 ，NVIDIA* 與 AMD* 
的 新 系 統 展 示 偏 少 ， 許 多 ODM※ 與 
OEM※ 廠商擺出來的機櫃 ， 和 Computex 
展覽上看到的差不多 。 但如果仔細看各家的
進度 ， 會發現重點在於 「什麼時候開始賣」 。
輝達 VR200 機櫃已進入小量出貨階段 ， 之
後還有搭配  Groq 3 LPX 的機櫃，預計  
2026 年底或  2027 年 初推出，而只放 
Vera CPU 的獨立機櫃則要等到  2027 年
第 1 季末或第 2 季初。AMD 的 Helios 
機櫃 幾乎沒有改款，量產時間預期落在  
2027 年第 1 季。 
從試產機櫃的觀察來看 ， 供應鏈名單也逐漸
浮現，AI 伺服器的商機正在往下游的零組
件延伸，而不只是晶片本身。 
※ODM (Original Design Manufacturer) 原廠委託設計代
工 ， 替品牌廠設計並生產產品的廠商 ， 例如 鴻海*、緯穎*。 
※OEM (Original Equipment Manufacturer) 原廠委託代
工，依品牌廠提供的設計進行生產。 
圖二：OCP 展上 AMD Helios 伺服器

資料來源：OCP，AMD，2026/10/18 
▋ Maia 300 自研晶片野心 
微軟在大會上公開 Maia 300，這是它自家
推論 (inference)※ 基礎設施的重大擴張，
預計 2027 年開始實際部署，長期目標是
把 Maia 系列擴大到數個  GW 的規模。
從規格來看 ， 每個機櫃包含 96 顆加速器 、
24 個運算機盤，以及分布在  3 個液冷交
換機盤中的 6 顆交換器晶片，總共搭載約 
27.6 TB 的 HBM，整個機櫃的供電規格約
為 167 kW。 網路方面採用兩層乙太網路架
構，最多可串接 128 個機櫃，而液冷系統
能帶走機櫃超過 80% 的熱量。 
在效益上 ，微軟 Maia 300 每百萬瓦能產出
的 token 數量 ，比領先的 GPU 系統高出 
40%，機櫃層級的 token 處理量最高是前
一代 Maia 200 的 7 倍。不過要注意，這
個 7 倍的數字是在機櫃內加速器數量增
為 2 倍的前提下得出的，不能完全視為單
顆晶片的進步。若以 PUE※ 1.1 至 1.2 估
算，1 GW 的資料中心大約需要 5,000 至 
5,500 個這樣的機櫃 ， 市場規模相當可觀 。 
※PUE (Power Usage Effectiveness) 電力使用效率，數值
越接近 1 代表越省電 ，1.1 至 1.2 屬於非常優秀的水準 。 
圖三：Microsoft 推出客製化晶片伺服器

資料來源：微軟、北富銀整理，2026/10/19

▋ 電網需求暴增，供應鏈受惠 
輝達在 Rubin Ultra 上評估使用 PTFE※材
料的電路板，原本是配合 Kyber 機櫃架構，
但因為 Kyber 延後，評估重心又回到現行
的 Oberon 架構。PTFE 的優點是訊號傳
輸表現更好、電氣特性穩定，也比較不必依
賴 Q 玻纖布※ 這類稀缺材料。缺點則是
加工很複雜，成本高 ，良率與認證也都是挑
戰，所以什麼時候大規模採用還說不準。 
※PTFE (聚四氟乙烯) 一種訊號損耗低的特殊材料，也就是
俗稱的鐵氟龍，用在高速電路板上可以減少訊號衰減。 
※Q 玻纖布 (Q glass) 一種用於高階電路板的低損耗玻纖
材料，供給相對有限，價格也較高。 
※NVLink 輝達開發的專有高速互聯技術 ， 專為 GPU 之間
的高頻寬通訊設計。 
輝達在大會上展示超高密度連接器  Ultra 
Dense Interconnect (UDI)※，目標是取代 
安費諾(Amphenol) 的 HD2 產品，應用
在中板  (midplane) 與纜線匣  (cable 
cartridge) 上。業界認為導入時間還要約  
2 年 ， 現階段供應商正集中精力提升良率 ，
並把價格壓到 100 美元以下，其中彈簧針 
(pogo-pin) 的供應是瓶頸。 
在散熱端 ，輝達新推出的 ZQD※ 快速接頭 ，
主打單位面積內能通過更大的液體流量 ， 現
場共展示 14 家供應商。因為設計比較複
雜 ， 預期它的平均售價會比目前 20 至 30 
美元的快速接頭高出一截 ， 雖然廠商仍持續
努力改善良率並降低成本。

※UDI (Ultra Dense Interconnect) 超高密度互連，在更小
空間內塞進更多訊號接點的連接器。 
※ZQD 液冷系統中用來連接或拆卸水管的快速接頭，可以
在不漏水的情況下插拔，是液冷機櫃的關鍵零件。

圖四：NVIDIA PTFE 主板

資料來源：NVIDIA，MS，2026/10/19

▋ 結論：受惠者擴散 
OCP 大會傳達的訊息是  AI 硬體的成長
動能依然存在 ， 但受惠對象正由晶片擴散到
機櫃周邊 。 對投資人而言 ， 有幾個觀察重點 。
首先是時程 ，VR200、Helios 與 Maia 300 
的量產節奏集中在 2026 年底到 2027 年，
這段期間各家出貨進度的變化會直接影響
相關供應鏈的營收預期 。 其次是良率 ，PTFE 
電路板、UDI 連接器、ZQD 快速接頭都屬
於新技術 ，價格雖然較高 ，但若良率改善太
慢，利潤不一定能同步放大。 
AI 投資主軸由 「晶片驅動」 邁向 「基礎設施
驅動」 。 過去市場聚焦 GPU、ASIC 等核心
運算晶片 ， 未來則將進一步擴散至 AI 機櫃 、
液冷散熱 、電力設備 、高速網路及光通訊等
受惠領域。換言之，AI 投資的下一階段不
再只是比拼晶片算力 ， 而是比拚整體資料中
心系統的建置能力與運行效率 ， 相關基礎建
設供應鏈可望迎來新的投資機會。

*所列個股為舉例說明，並非個股推薦

公司拜訪快報

達發(6526)：AI 基礎建設加速有線網通成長 
建議：維持買進

一 、 達發與母公司聯發科為大小
平台、分工合作的關係，選擇投
資有迭代、有門檻的技術 
 達發是由母公司聯發科的物聯網部門，加上絡達、創發、原睿，三個公司、四
個團隊組合在一起的。專注於網通基礎建設與高階 AI 物聯網，包含有線通
訊、無線通訊兩大領域、四個晶片系列的 IC 設計公司。達發是聯發科集團布
局的重要一環。聯發科的晶片多為上千人研發團隊規模所開發的大平台，達發
的晶片則是百人規模小平台，雙方是大小平台、分工合作的關係，可以提供更
高價值、更完整的方案，服務全球客戶。2026 年全系列晶片導入 AI 技術。

二、應用於 Edge AI 的近程無
線晶片長期朝高階市場發展，
6nm 平台將於 2026 年底完成
設計定案 
 近程無線晶片產品線主要應用於 Edge AI ，包括藍牙音訊、藍牙通訊與衛星定
位晶片，與母公司大平台晶片 5G、Wi-Fi、車用等分工合作，可回應更趨複雜
且多元終端融合使用的新需求，是支援最後一哩路創新應用的基礎。因有線網
通成長力道更強勁，近程無線晶片 2024 年佔營收 55%，2025~2026 年降為
50%。 
藍牙音訊晶片持續維持品牌客戶旗艦產品的採用外，也已延伸至電競、商務、
助輔聽，助輔聽已切入全球前五大品牌，已開始供應 OTC，未來規劃開發醫
療等級。面對規格持續迭代的終端融合使用新需求，提供更豐富的 AI 音訊方
案。主攻歐美日國際高端品牌，商務應用 2026 年開始收割。BTA 可服務的市
場規模預估 10~20 億美元。新產品線藍牙通訊 BTD，應用產品包括遙控器、
Tracker、ESL 等，已切入全球前三大電競品牌，未來持續拓展至 B2B 市場。 
衛星定位晶片切入全球前五大智慧手錶品牌客戶。除了持續維持頂規穿戴裝置
品牌客戶的採用，面對未來穿戴、行業應用、車用等多元應用領域，持續提供
定位快、定位準、最省電的解決方案。達發的衛星定位晶片已於車商選配、車
用後裝等追蹤應用為車廠採用，並與聯發科 Dimensity Auto 車用平台完成系統
整合。

三、有線網通因 AI 基礎建設快
速成長，寬頻及乙太網切入 AI
資料中心 BMC 帶外管理 
 有線網通產品線包括光纖固網寬頻、乙太網及光通訊晶片。 2024 年佔營收
35%，隨著乙太網與光通高速成長，2025 年營收佔比提升至 45%，2026 年持
續提升至 50%，預估 2027 年持續提升。 
寬頻產品線目前主力仍為 GPON，全球市佔率約 17%，10GPON 全球市佔率
快速提升，已佔寬頻營收 30%。全球超過 30 家運營商採用，在歐洲拿到一線
電信商標案，2025 年開始量產貢獻營收。 10GPON 目前滲透率 20%，預估
2026 年將持續提升至 30%。10GPON 於歐洲顯著成長，2026 年加速北美市
場布局， 2026 年前三季北美營收倍增，預估歐洲與北美將佔營收 50%。
25GPON 已送樣美系客戶驗證，預計 2027 年進入量產。將持續推進 50GPON
具備 PQC，搶攻 AI 資料中心市場。

公司拜訪快報 
乙太網營收貢獻度超過 10%，透過策略參股與九暘縱向垂直延伸 GbE 以下的
FE (Fast Ethernet) 及 PoE (Power over Ethernet) 完整產品線，橫向則與亞信
專長的 PC 介面乙太網合作，共同發揮三家公司產品技術優勢。集團轉投資
Aeosemi，完善 10G Ethernet PHY 布局。乙太網市場策略將由市佔率擴張進
階至規格提升。積極耕耘低軌衛星應用，包括 Dish 戶外接收機 Dish 與室內路
由器設備，目前低軌衛星主要客戶已成為乙太網最大客戶，且規格持續由 1G
提升至 2.5G。非管理型及輕管理型 switch 市占率持續提升。 
此外，寬頻與乙太網已切入北美大型 CSP 供應鏈，支援 BMC 帶外管理(Out-
of-Band Management)，預估 2026~2028 年市場規模將逐年翻倍成長。"

四、光通 2026 年與 2027 年營
收指引皆為數倍成長 
 超高速 SerDes 傳輸及光驅動 Laser Driver 技術，已開發出 50/100/200/400 
Gbps PAM4 DSP+LDD 系列晶片方案，主要應用於數據中心設備間互連的高
速光模組。2024 年主要終端客戶為美國 CSP，2025 年新增光模組客戶，終端
客戶擴張至中系 CSP，2026 年營收貢獻度預估 4%~6%，目前仍在快速起飛
階段。周邊 TIA 採用矽鍺製程，單價為主晶片 1/3~1/4，2025 年已開始搭配既
有客戶的 50G 主晶片量產。單通道 100G 主晶片已於 4Q25 設計定案，獲得全
球前五大光模組客戶 Design-in，2027 年開始貢獻營收；搭配之 TIA 同步推
出，搭載率將大幅提升。預估 2028 年之前 100G 八通道之 800G 模組仍將為
市場主流，下一世代 1.6T 模組，單通道 200G 主晶片+TIA 相關之研發人員及
IP 已到位，將同時提供可插拔與 NPO 方案。光通訊產品線毛利率為產品線中
最高，亦為成長率最大的產品線。NPO/CPO 與可插拔光通模組互補並進，非
直接取代。近期完成轉投資元澄，取得 2.1%股權，擴大光通版圖。 
九暘的 POE 於安防工控市場具備高度潛力。九暘與 STB 業務毛利率低於公司
平均。

五、3Q26 營收續創歷史新高，
毛利率季減、營業利益率及淨
利率季增；4Q26 指引季增、年
增 
 有 線 無 線 兩 大 產 品 線 皆 成 長 ，3Q26 營收 69.41 億 元 ，+10.4%QOQ 、
+23.3%YOY，續創歷史新高。毛利率則因成本上漲(主要來自 KGD、NOR 及
OSAT)，季減1.9 個百分點為 49.3%。營業利益率季增 1.3 個百分點為 16.3%；
營業利益 11.34 億元，+20.2%QOQ、+29.3%YOY。稅後淨利 10.38 億元，
+17.9%QOQ、+23.9%YOY，EPS 6.18 元。 
4Q26 指引：光通、乙太網、藍牙持續成長，營收將維持季增表現、年增幅度
加大。 
2026 年指引：營收持續成長，光通、乙太網與九暘合計營收將佔 20%；高毛
利率產品線，光通、低軌衛星、AI 資料中心相關營收貢獻度合計，2025 年低
於 5%，2026 年將提升至 5%~10%。透過產品組合提升，以及價格調整反映
成本，毛利率預估維持 50%以上。營業費用年增 10%。"

六、維持買進理由  AI 基礎建設加速有線網通成長，光通、寬頻、乙太網切入 CSP 資料中心。
